Oh, well, I wouldn't say I feel *worthless*, exactly. I mean, I know I do good work with my clients, and I try to be a good person... I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around throwing a pity party for myself.

I hope it's okay to admit that? I just feel bad complaining when I'm supposed to be the one taking care of people. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than you usually do?\n\nPatient: Actually, um... no, I don't think so. I still really enjoy my usual hobbies and things. I like to work in my garden on the weekends, and that still feels just as nice as it always does. \n\nI try really hard to make sure I'm still doing things that make me smile, just so I don't let that... that heavy feeling take over completely. I hope that's a good sign? I mean, it's probably a good thing that I can still enjoy my time off, right?\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my sleep hasn't been perfect, I suppose. I just want to make sure I'm not making a big deal out of nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have these memory or concentration problems started to interfere with your work?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I just want to make sure I'm not letting anything slip through the cracks. Well, um... I wouldn't say it's making it *hard* to function, exactly. I mean, I'm still going to work every day, still taking care of my clients. I would never just stop doing my job, you know? They really need me.

Oh, no, definitely not. Um... I know sometimes people isolate themselves when they're grieving, but I really try not to do that. It wouldn't be fair to my wife or my friends to just shut them out, you know?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm completely drained or anything like that. I still get up and go to work, and I try my best to keep up with everything. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining or just being lazy. 

But, I guess... well, I have noticed that even on days when I don't do a whole lot, my body just feels... kind of worn out. Like, even simple things at work, like helping one of my clients with a shower, or just bringing the groceries in at home... it just seems to take a lot more out of me than it usually does. 

I know grief can take a physical toll on a person, right? It's pretty normal to feel a little more tired when you're going through something emotional, isn't it? I'm just trying to push through it and make sure I'm still doing what I need to do. I hope that makes sense.

I mean, I still get enough rest to do my job, so it's not a huge problem or anything. I hope it's normal to have a few rough nights here and there? I'm sure everybody deals with that sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I worry a *lot*. I mean, I care about my family and my clients, so of course I think about them and want to make sure everyone is doing okay. That's just part of who I am, I think. \n\nI try really hard not to let things get to me too much. Sometimes my mind might get a little busy, like I mentioned with the sleeping, but I don't think it's anything excessive. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and being there for the people who need me. That's a pretty normal amount of worrying, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not making a big deal out of nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have these memory or concentration problems started to interfere with your work?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's interfering, exactly. I mean, I'm still getting everything done, and my clients are all taken care of. I just... I want to make sure I'm doing things right, you know? \n\nSo, um, I have been asking some of my coworkers to just double-check my documentation lately. Just to be safe, because I want to be thorough. And, well, at home, my wife has been helping out a little more with the bills and the scheduling. I just haven't felt quite as sharp with all those little details, so she offered to take over for a bit. It's really nice of her, and I think it's good to ask for help when you need it, right? I just want to make sure I'm not letting anything slip through the cracks. That's a responsible way to handle it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Did this foggy feeling start after something particularly stressful or upsetting happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess you could say that, yeah. It hasn't been going on forever or anything. Just, um... well, my uncle passed away about two weeks ago. \n\nIt was a bit of a shock, and, you know, we were pretty close. So I think it's just natural to feel a little off after something like that, right? Anyone would be a bit distracted or foggy while they're processing a loss. I just... I really think it's just a normal reaction to grief, don't you? It's only been a couple of weeks, so I'm sure it'll pass once things settle down a bit.\n\nDoctor: Since your uncle died, have you been finding yourself longing for him or preoccupied with thoughts of him?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say that. I'll think about the good memories we had sometimes, sure, but it's definitely not a preoccupation. I'm just trying to keep moving forward and handle it the best I can. That's the healthiest way to grieve, isn't it? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm falling apart or letting it take over my life. I think I'm managing it pretty normally, right?\n\nDoctor: Since your uncle died, have you felt sad or down for most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Like, I can put on a smile for my clients, but inside I just feel kind of sad and... flat, I guess. But I think that's pretty normal when you're grieving, right? It's okay to feel a little sad for a while after losing someone, don't you think? I just wouldn't want you to worry that I'm not handling it well, because I really am trying my best to stay positive.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, I would never... I'm usually such a positive person. That's just the grief talking, right? As long as I'm keeping my strength up, a little change in appetite isn't anything to worry about, is it? Um... no, I don't think so. I haven't really been feeling nervous or, you know, jittery at all.

As long as I'm keeping my strength up, a little change in appetite isn't anything to worry about, is it? I just want to make sure I'm doing all the right things to take care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or blaming yourself a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I wouldn't say I feel *worthless*, exactly. I mean, I know I do good work with my clients, and I try to be a good person... I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around throwing a pity party for myself. \n\nBut, um... I'm trying not to be too hard on myself, because I know he wouldn't want me to feel guilty. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm still doing all the things I usually do. I still like watching my shows in the evening and, you know, keeping up with my normal routine. 

Obviously, I'm grieving, so maybe I'm a little quieter than usual, but I haven't lost interest in my life or anything. I still get enjoyment out of my hobbies. I think it's really important to keep doing the things you love when you're going through a tough time, don't you? It helps keep you grounded. So, yeah, I'm still enjoying things just fine. That's a good sign, right?

Like at work, I've asked some of my coworkers to just double-check my client documentation for me.
